## Attempts already made

- INSTRUMENTS (29 Sept 2026, runner 12, H302-H329): a blind FREE letterform sort failed its own gate three times on LL (H302, H304 CONTROL FAIL; H309 NON-TEST) and is retired for that question (HYPOTHESES.md, untested-by-this-tool). A per-tile FORCED CHOICE with known answers passed where the free sort could not (H310 ONE/TWO/NEITHER 14/14; H311 LEADIN/UPRIGHT 11/11) and failed only where the known-answer tiles themselves were poor or confusable (H324: f.101r's dense page, both known tiles unclear; H322: VBAR_A and EBR confusable on f.211r's run). So: prefer the forced-choice design, check the known-answer tiles' centring on a placement sheet first, and never take known answers from a dense leaf without hand-set centres. For held-leaf checks of the key, the H325/H328 count (two-pass agreed gloss letters matched one-to-one into v7's cells) must be run against a frequency-matched control (binned permutation and a frequency-only key), not cells shuffled across all classes alone. BEAM CHECKS (runner 13, H335-H341): a beam score of v7 against within-frequency-bin permuted keys is not an order control -- its shuffled-order arm (ARM-C1) voided it on 4 of 7 leaves (H341); a beam gate needs the shuffled-target arm from its first run, and the frequency-only key is not a fair beam control (it fails in-sample, H336). TRANSCRIPTION FAULTS BY ORDER (runner 13, H358-H366): an order-gain pointer that a cell widening beats random widenings (H358) was confirmed by a gated shape forced choice (H193 anchors in every call), the split's order gain with a shuffled-target control, and the leaf's own period letters by shape answer (f.101r: no-bowl 4TRI -> a/n 118 of 130) -- the readers' 4TRI code is two signs; check a code's shape split against period letters before pooling it into a key. PART-2 BLIND SPOT (runner 14, H371/H373): the H359 design's gate sits on part 1 only, so a reader that answers part 2 by default passes it -- H371's chunk c2 answered 'no' to 37/37 at gate 18/20 and did not reproduce (fresh re-read 25/37, gate 17/20). Any chunk whose part-2 answers are all one value is re-read before use, and future bowl calls embed 4-6 known-answer strips cut in part-2 format (marker above) among the targets, scored as a second gate.

  prints only a partial decode, self-labelled "Solution Incomplete": one phrase, "jalousie au beau-pere", plus a handful

  brief's box/cap met its own stopping point once the gate outcome was diagnosed; no second vision call made (see H2
